A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

撒哈拉以南非洲地区 季节性流感 的发病率和 死亡率 负担:系统评价和荟萃分析方案

Morbidity and mortality burden of seasonal influenza in sub-Saharan Africa: a systematic review and meta-analysis protocol
作者： Marie Roseline Darnycka Belizaire, Anderson Kouabenan N’gattia, Dick Damas Chamla, Ibrahima Socé Fall, Yap Boum, Baidy Lô, Francisco Bolumar, Blondy Kayembe-Mulumba, Marie Roseline Darnycka Bélizaire, Baidy Lo
摘要中文翻译季节性流感对全球健康构成重大负担,每年导致大量发病率和死亡率,尤其是在高风险人群中,如老年人、婴幼儿、孕妇和患有慢性疾病的个体。尽管流感在高收入国家的负担已被充分记录,但撒哈拉以南非洲(SSA)国家的数据却十分有限,脆弱的医疗体系和报告不足掩盖了其真实影响。流感会加重普遍存在的健康状况,如HIV、疟疾和营养不良,进一步增加其危害。本协议中概述的荟萃分析旨在系统评估季节性流感在SSA国家的发病率和死亡率负担,为政策制定者提供关键见解,以加强监测、优先考虑高风险人群,并制定疫苗接种策略。这是一项关于计划进行的系统综述和荟萃分析的协议。本研究将遵循系统综述和荟萃分析的首选报告项目(PRISMA)2020指南,纳入2010年8月至2026年12月期间发表的观察性研究和监测报告。我们将搜索主要的健康数据库,包括MEDLINE(通过PubMed)、Embase、Scopus、Web of Science和Cochrane图书馆,以及区域数据库(非洲医学索引和世界卫生组织全球医学索引)和灰色文献来源(medRxiv和国家健康报告)。将纳入来自SSA国家的实验室确诊季节性流感病例的研究。将使用标准化表格进行数据提取,记录发病率(患病率、发病率和住院率)和死亡率(病例死亡率和超额死亡率)指标。将使用荟萃分析技术对汇总数据进行分析,应用随机效应模型以考虑异质性。敏感性分析将解决不同病例定义、方法和研究质量之间的差异。通过纳入灰色文献并不限制语言,将减轻出版偏倚和语言偏倚。完成的综述预计将在SSA国家提供更新的、汇总的季节性流感相关发病率和死亡率估计,按年龄组、地理次区域和合并症的存在情况进行分层。这些发现将支持基于证据的政策决策,包括加强流感监测系统和优先考虑SSA国家高风险人群的疫苗接种项目。由于本研究涉及已发表文献的二次数据,无需伦理批准。结果将通过同行评审的出版物、会议报告和利益相关者会议进行传播。将为SSA国家的政策制定者提供定制报告,将研究结果转化为公共卫生策略,特别是增强监测并实施针对性的疫苗接种项目。PROSPERO CRD42024617707
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
季节性流感对全球健康构成重大负担,每年导致大量发病率和死亡率,尤其是在高风险人群中,如老年人、婴幼儿、孕妇和患有慢性疾病的个体。本协议中概述的荟萃分析旨在系统评估季节性流感在SSA国家的发病率和死亡率负担,为政策制定者提供关键见解,以加强监测、优先考虑高风险人群,并制定疫苗接种策略。
证据基础与方法
本研究将遵循系统综述和荟萃分析的首选报告项目(PRISMA)2020指南,纳入2010年8月至2026年12月期间发表的观察性研究和监测报告。我们将搜索主要的健康数据库,包括MEDLINE(通过PubMed)、Embase、Scopus、Web of Science和Cochrane图书馆,以及区域数据库(非洲医学索引和世界卫生组织全球医学索引)和灰色文献来源(medRxiv和国家健康报告)。将纳入来自SSA国家的实验室确诊季节性流感病例的研究。
共识与结论
完成的综述预计将在SSA国家提供更新的、汇总的季节性流感相关发病率和死亡率估计,按年龄组、地理次区域和合并症的存在情况进行分层。流感会加重普遍存在的健康状况,如HIV、疟疾和营养不良,进一步增加其危害。
争议与缺口
提供的证据未报告关于SSA国家季节性流感负担的具体汇总估计或稳定结论。综述协议概述了方法,但未包含实质性结果或解释。证据未详细讨论异质性或冲突发现。提供的证据未涉及关于SSA国家季节性流感负担的未解决机制或具体证据缺口。
科研与实践启示
这些发现将支持基于证据的政策决策,包括加强流感监测系统和优先考虑SSA国家高风险人群的疫苗接种项目。将为SSA国家的政策制定者提供定制报告,将研究结果转化为公共卫生策略,特别是增强监测并实施针对性的疫苗接种项目。

Morbidity and mortality burden of seasonal influenza in sub-Saharan Africa: a systematic review and meta-analysis protocol

Authors: Marie Roseline Darnycka Belizaire, Anderson Kouabenan N’gattia, Dick Damas Chamla, Ibrahima Socé Fall, Yap Boum, Baidy Lô, Francisco Bolumar, Blondy Kayembe-Mulumba, Marie Roseline Darnycka Bélizaire, Baidy Lo
Original abstract (English)
Seasonal influenza poses a significant global health burden, responsible for substantial morbidity and mortality annually, particularly among high-risk populations such as the elderly, young children, pregnant women, and individuals with chronic illnesses. While the burden of influenza is well-documented in high-income countries, limited data exist for sub-Saharan African (SSA) countries, where fragile healthcare systems and underreporting obscure the true impact. Influenza exacerbates prevalent health conditions such as HIV, malaria, and malnutrition, further increasing its toll. The meta-analysis outlined in this protocol aims to systematically assess the morbidity and mortality burden of seasonal influenza in SSA countries, providing policymakers with critical insights to strengthen surveillance, prioritize at-risk populations, and inform vaccination strategies. This is a protocol for a planned systematic review and meta-analysis. This research will follow the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses (PRISMA) 2020 guidelines, incorporating observational studies and surveillance reports published between August 2010 and December 2026. We will search major health databases, including MEDLINE (via PubMed), Embase, Scopus, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Library, as well as regional databases (African Index Medicus and the WHO Global Index Medicus) and grey literature sources (medRxiv and national health reports). Studies reporting laboratory-confirmed seasonal influenza cases from SSA countries will be included. A standardized form will be used for data extraction, capturing morbidity (prevalence, incidence, and hospitalization rates) and mortality (case-fatality rates and excess mortality rates) indicators. Pooled data will be analyzed using meta-analytic techniques, applying random-effects models to account for heterogeneity. Sensitivity analyses will address variations in case definitions, methodologies, and study quality across studies. Publication and language bias will be mitigated by including grey literature and applying no language restrictions. The completed review is expected to provide updated, pooled estimates of seasonal influenza-associated morbidity and mortality across SSA countries, stratified by age group, geographic subregion, and presence of comorbidities. These findings will support evidence-based policy decisions, including the strengthening of influenza surveillance systems and the prioritization of vaccination programs for high-risk populations in SSA countries. As this study involves secondary data from published literature, ethical approval is not required. Results will be disseminated via a peer-reviewed publication, conference presentations, and stakeholder meetings. Tailored reports will be shared with policymakers in SSA countries to translate findings into public health strategies, particularly to enhance surveillance and implement targeted vaccination programs. PROSPERO CRD42024617707
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Seasonal influenza poses a significant global health burden, responsible for substantial morbidity and mortality annually, particularly among high-risk populations such as the elderly, young children, pregnant women, and individuals with chronic illnesses. The meta-analysis outlined in this protocol aims to systematically assess the morbidity and mortality burden of seasonal influenza in SSA countries, providing policymakers with critical insights to strengthen surveillance, prioritize at-risk populations, and inform vaccination strategies.
Evidence base and method
This research will follow the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses (PRISMA) 2020 guidelines, incorporating observational studies and surveillance reports published between August 2010 and December 2026. We will search major health databases, including MEDLINE (via PubMed), Embase, Scopus, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Library, as well as regional databases (African Index Medicus and the WHO Global Index Medicus) and grey literature sources (medRxiv and national health reports). Studies reporting laboratory-confirmed seasonal influenza cases from SSA countries will be included.
Consensus and conclusions
The completed review is expected to provide updated, pooled estimates of seasonal influenza-associated morbidity and mortality across SSA countries, stratified by age group, geographic subregion, and presence of comorbidities. Influenza exacerbates prevalent health conditions such as HIV, malaria, and malnutrition, further increasing its toll.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report specific pooled estimates or stable conclusions about the burden of seasonal influenza in SSA countries. The review protocol outlines the methodology but does not include substantive results or interpretations. The evidence does not address heterogeneity or conflicting findings in detail. The supplied evidence does not report on unresolved mechanisms or concrete evidence gaps related to the burden of seasonal influenza in SSA countries.
Research and practice implications
These findings will support evidence-based policy decisions, including the strengthening of influenza surveillance systems and the prioritization of vaccination programs for high-risk populations in SSA countries. Tailored reports will be shared with policymakers in SSA countries to translate findings into public health strategies, particularly to enhance surveillance and implement targeted vaccination programs.

破译乙型流感病毒衍生肽及其通过 HLA I 类分子的呈递。

Deciphering influenza B virus-derived peptides and their presentation by HLA class I molecules.
作者： Janesha C Maddumage, Georgia R Dow, Lawton D Murdolo, Samuel Liwei Leong, Demetra S M Chatzileontiadou, Emma J Grant, Stephanie Gras
摘要中文翻译尽管流感 疫苗 已广泛使用,但流感仍然每年导致显着的发病率和 死亡率。疫苗 通常会诱导体液介导的针对快速突变表面糖蛋白的保护,因此需要每年更新和施用它们。相比之下,可以控制和清除病毒感染的CD8+ T细胞可以识别更保守的病毒表位。因此,人们对了解 CD8+ T 细胞对流感病毒的反应有很大兴趣,以开发未来的 疫苗 和治疗方法。尽管甲型流感病毒 (FLUAV) 和甲型流感病毒 (FLUBV) 在人类中共同传播,并且都导致季节性流行,但有关 CD8+ T 细胞对 FLUBV 反应的数据有限。这种知识差距涵盖了免疫学和分子学方面的见解。在本综述中,我们总结了目前对 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位在细胞和分子水平上的了解,并与 FLUAV 进行了比较。总的来说,这凸显了有关 FLUBV 的可用数据有限,尽管它在人类流感感染中发挥着重要作用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述检查了人类 季节性流感 B 病毒 (FLUBV),重点是 CD8+ T 细胞对保守表位的反应,与甲型流感病毒 (FLUAV) 进行对比,并解决了这些反应如何为未来的 疫苗 和治疗提供信息的核心问题。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了细胞和分子水平上 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位的最新知识,明确比较了 FLUBV 与 FLUAV,并强调了有关 FLUBV 的可用数据有限,尽管它在人类流感感染中发挥着作用。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,CD8+ T 细胞比体液反应识别更保守的病毒表位,从而提供了更广泛保护的潜力,但指出与 FLUAV 相比,FLUBV 特异性 CD8+ T 细胞表位数据仍然稀疏。
争议与缺口
该综述指出了关于 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位的重大知识差距,涵盖免疫学和分子见解,并强调在这方面缺乏 FLUBV 和 FLUAV 之间的比较数据。
科研与实践启示
该综述优先研究表征 FLUBV 衍生的 CD8+ T 细胞表位及其 HLA I 类分子的呈递,旨在指导 疫苗 和利用保守 T 细胞反应的疗法的开发。

Deciphering influenza B virus-derived peptides and their presentation by HLA class I molecules.

Authors: Janesha C Maddumage, Georgia R Dow, Lawton D Murdolo, Samuel Liwei Leong, Demetra S M Chatzileontiadou, Emma J Grant, Stephanie Gras
Original abstract (English)
Despite influenza vaccines being widely available, influenza still causes significant morbidity and mortality annually. Vaccines typically induce humoral-mediated protection against rapidly mutating surface glycoproteins, necessitating that they be updated and administered each year. In contrast, CD8+ T cells, which can control and clear viral infections, can recognise more conserved viral epitopes. Therefore, there is considerable interest in understanding CD8+ T cell responses to influenza virus for the development of future vaccines and therapeutics. Although Alphainfluenzavirus influenzae (FLUAV) and Betainfluenzavirus influenzae (FLUBV) co-circulate in humans and both contribute to seasonal epidemics, there is limited data regarding CD8+ T cell responses to FLUBV. This knowledge gap spans both immunological and molecular insights. In the present review, we summarise the current knowledge of FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes at both cellular and molecular levels, in comparison with FLUAV. Collectively, this highlights the limited data available on FLUBV, despite its significant role in human influenza infections.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines human seasonal influenza B virus (FLUBV) with a focus on CD8+ T cell responses to conserved epitopes, contrasting with influenza A virus (FLUAV) and addressing the central question of how these responses could inform future vaccines and therapeutics.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes current knowledge on FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes at cellular and molecular levels, explicitly comparing FLUBV to FLUAV, and highlights the limited data available on FLUBV despite its role in human influenza infections.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that CD8+ T cells recognize more conserved viral epitopes than humoral responses, offering potential for broader protection, but notes that FLUBV-specific CD8+ T cell epitope data remain sparse compared to FLUAV.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies a significant knowledge gap regarding FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes, spanning both immunological and molecular insights, and emphasizes the lack of comparative data between FLUBV and FLUAV in this context.
Research and practice implications
The review prioritizes research to characterize FLUBV-derived CD8+ T cell epitopes and their presentation by HLA class I molecules, aiming to guide the development of vaccines and therapeutics that leverage conserved T cell responses.

医院就诊患者中细菌共感染及抗菌药物使用情况:一项系统综述和荟萃分析

Bacterial co-infection and antimicrobial use in hospital-attended patients with laboratory-confirmed influenza infection: a systematic review and meta-analysis.
作者： Bott, H., Pei, R., Murphy, M. E., Shi, T., Ho, A.
摘要中文翻译背景流感相关细菌共感染显著增加发病率和死亡率。COVID-19大流行期间,病毒流行模式、诊断检测与抗菌药物使用模式发生显著变化,但当代流感患者细菌共感染与抗菌药物使用的估计值尚未被综合分析。目的估计实验室确诊流感住院患者中微生物学确认的细菌共感染患病率的合并患病率。次要目标包括:表征共感染细菌病原体、量化整体抗菌药物处方比例及关键亚组间差异。本研究已在PROSPERO注册(CRD420251072782)。数据来源与纳入标准检索Embase(Ovid)、MEDLINE、PubMed、Scopus及Web of Science至2025年6月15日,纳入研究需满足以下条件:住院患者≥50例且实验室确诊流感,并报告细菌共感染。方法采用广义线性混合模型(logit链接函数)计算合并患病率估计值与抗菌药物处方比例。亚组分析包括年龄组、临床设置及季节性vs.全年性流感。采用JBI关键评估工具评估偏倚风险,并使用GRADE评估证据质量。结果共纳入来自30个国家的97项研究,涉及116,273例流感患者,其中10,880例确诊细菌共感染。细菌共感染的合并患病率为16.7%(95%CI 13.9-20.0%;I2=99.0%)。ICU患者的患病率高于非ICU患者(27.6% vs. 最常见的细菌病原体为肺炎链球菌(35.7%)和金黄色葡萄球菌(30.3%)。在38项研究中报告的抗菌药物使用率较高(合并患病率为88.1%,95%CI 76.0-94.5%;I2=99.9%),成人使用率高于儿童(97.8% vs 65.0%),ICU患者高于非ICU患者(96% vs 81%)。结论在约六分之一的住院流感病例中发现细菌共感染,但抗菌药物处方却近乎普遍。研究间存在显著异质性与诊断差异,限制了结果解释,但凸显了临床决策面临的持续挑战。研究结果支持加强诊断能力建设与抗菌药物管理,以优化疑似流感相关细菌共感染的管理。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究旨在估计医院就诊且实验室确诊流感患者中微生物学确认的细菌共感染的汇总患病率,并描述共感染的细菌病原体及量化总体和关键亚组中的抗菌药物处方比例。
证据基础与方法
本综述采用系统方法进行,检索了Embase(Ovid)、MEDLINE、PubMed、Scopus和Web of Science,截止至2025年6月15日。纳入标准为包含≥50名医院就诊且实验室确诊流感的患者,并报告了细菌共感染的研究。使用广义线性混合模型(logit链接)计算汇总患病率估计值和抗菌药物处方比例。使用JBI批判性评价工具评估偏倚风险,并使用GRADE评估证据的确定性。
共识与结论
医院就诊且实验室确诊流感患者中细菌共感染的汇总患病率为16.7%(95%CI 13.9-20.0%;I2=99.0%)。最常鉴定的细菌病原体为肺炎链球菌(35.7%)和金黄色葡萄球菌(30.3%)。抗菌药物使用率很高(汇总患病率为88.1%,95%CI 76.0-94.5%;I2=99.9%),在成人中比儿童更高,在ICU环境中比非ICU环境更高。
争议与缺口
注意到显著的异质性和诊断差异,这限制了解释并突显了临床决策中的持续挑战。现有证据未提供关于细菌共感染具体机制或其对疾病结局影响的详细信息。
科研与实践启示
这些发现支持加强诊断能力和抗菌药物管理,以优化疑似流感相关细菌共感染的管理。

Bacterial co-infection and antimicrobial use in hospital-attended patients with laboratory-confirmed influenza infection: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Authors: Bott, H., Pei, R., Murphy, M. E., Shi, T., Ho, A.
Original abstract (English)
BackgroundBacterial co-infection contributes substantially to influenza-associated morbidity and mortality. Patterns of viral circulation, diagnostic testing and antimicrobial use changed markedly during the COVID-19 pandemic, yet contemporary estimates of bacterial co-infection and antimicrobial use in influenza have not been synthesised. ObjectivesTo estimate the pooled prevalence of microbiologically confirmed bacterial co-infection among hospital-attended patients with laboratory-confirmed influenza. Secondary objectives were to characterise co-infecting bacterial pathogens, quantify antimicrobial prescribing overall and across key subgroups. This study was registered with PROSPERO (CRD420251072782). Data sources and eligibilityWe searched Embase (Ovid), MEDLINE, PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science to 15th June 2025 for studies including [≥]50 hospital-attended patients with laboratory-confirmed influenza and reporting bacterial co-infection. MethodsPooled prevalence estimates and antimicrobial prescription proportions were calculated using a generalised linear mixed model with logit link. Subgroup analyses included age group, clinical setting, and seasonal vs. Risk of bias was assessed using JBI Critical Appraisal tools and certainty of evidence using GRADE. ResultsNinety-seven studies from 30 countries, comprising 116,273 patients with influenza, met inclusion criteria; 10,880 had confirmed bacterial co-infection. The pooled prevalence was 16.7% (95%CI 13.9-20.0%; I2=99.0%). Prevalence was higher in ICU compared to non-ICU settings (27.6% vs. The most frequently identified bacterial pathogens were Streptococcus pneumoniae (35.7%) and Staphylococcus aureus (30.3%). Antimicrobial use, reported in 38 studies, was high (pooled prevalence 88.1%, 95%CI 76.0-94.5%; I2=99.9%), and was more common in adults than children (97.8% vs 65.0%), and in ICU compared with non-ICU settings (96% vs 81%). ConclusionsBacterial co-infection was identified in approximately one in six hospital-attended influenza cases, yet antimicrobial prescribing is near-universal. Substantial heterogeneity and diagnostic variability constraint interpretation but underscore persistent challenges in clinical decision-making. These findings support strengthened diagnostic capacity and antimicrobial stewardship to optimise management of suspected influenza-associated bacterial co-infection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study aimed to estimate the pooled prevalence of microbiologically confirmed bacterial co-infection among hospital-attended patients with laboratory-confirmed influenza, and to characterise co-infecting bacterial pathogens and quantify antimicrobial prescribing overall and across key subgroups.
Evidence base and method
The review was conducted using a systematic approach, searching Embase (Ovid), MEDLINE, PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science up to 15th June 2025. Studies were included if they had ≥50 hospital-attended patients with laboratory-confirmed influenza and reported bacterial co-infection. Pooled prevalence estimates and antimicrobial prescription proportions were calculated using a generalised linear mixed model with logit link. Risk of bias was assessed using JBI Critical Appraisal tools and certainty of evidence using GRADE.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled prevalence of bacterial co-infection among hospital-attended patients with laboratory-confirmed influenza was 16.7% (95%CI 13.9-20.0%; I2=99.0%). The most frequently identified bacterial pathogens were Streptococcus pneumoniae (35.7%) and Staphylococcus aureus (30.3%). Antimicrobial use was high (pooled prevalence 88.1%, 95%CI 76.0-94.5%; I2=99.9%), with higher rates observed in adults compared to children and in ICU settings compared to non-ICU settings.
Controversies and gaps
Substantial heterogeneity and diagnostic variability were noted, which constrain interpretation and highlight persistent challenges in clinical decision-making. The evidence does not provide detailed information on the specific mechanisms of bacterial co-infection or its impact on disease outcomes.
Research and practice implications
The findings support the need for strengthened diagnostic capacity and antimicrobial stewardship to optimise management of suspected influenza-associated bacterial co-infection.

基于M2e的纳米颗粒疫苗疫苗的理性设计以实现对甲型流感的通用保护

Rational design of M2e-based nanoparticle vaccines for universal influenza a protection
作者： Louis Bourlon, Jessica Morlieras, Patrice N. Marche, Fabrice P. Navarro
摘要中文翻译引言:甲型流感病毒因其抗原变异性和大流行潜力,持续对全球公共卫生和经济造成挑战。当前针对可变血凝素(HA)的季节性疫苗疫苗仅能提供有限且株特异性的保护。保守的基质蛋白2胞外结构域(M2e)作为一种有前景的通用疫苗靶标,通过靶向感染细胞而非中和病毒颗粒来诱导保护。然而,该简易抗原介导的保护机制复杂,且尚无基于M2e的疫苗获批上市。覆盖范围:亟需精准优化M2e-疫苗。本综述深入阐述M2e的已知知识,并对比基于M2e的疫苗研究。基于此,将界定通用疫苗(包括M2e)的“理想原型”。最后,将提供、描述并分析当前基于纳米颗粒的M2e 疫苗的临床前研究。专家意见:优化抗原呈递、通过标准化检测评估Fc介导的效应功能,并将M2e整合至靶向互补保守抗原的多价纳米颗粒平台,可能是实现广泛、持久且临床可转化保护的关键步骤。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
甲型流感病毒因其抗原变异性和大流行潜力而持续对全球健康和经济造成挑战。当前针对可变血凝素(HA)的季节性疫苗仅提供有限且株特异性的保护。保守的基质蛋白2胞外结构域(M2e)提供了一个极具前景的通用疫苗靶标,通过靶向感染细胞而非中和病毒颗粒来诱导保护。本综述旨在界定通用疫苗的“理想原型”,包括M2e,并回顾当前基于纳米颗粒的M2e 疫苗的临床前研究。
证据基础与方法
本综述全面阐述M2e的已知知识,并对比研究基于M2e的疫苗。综述涵盖该简单抗原介导的保护机制的讨论,并指出亟需精准优化M2e疫苗。
共识与结论
然而,该简单抗原介导的保护机制复杂,且尚无基于M2e的疫苗获批上市。本综述界定了包含M2e的通用疫苗“理想原型”。
争议与缺口
本综述强调了亟需精准优化M2e疫苗,并指出采用标准化检测评估Fc介导的效应功能的重要性。
科研与实践启示
优化抗原呈递、采用标准化检测评估Fc介导的效应功能,以及将M2e整合至针对互补保守抗原的多价纳米颗粒平台,可能是实现广泛、持久且临床可转化保护的关键步骤。

Rational design of M2e-based nanoparticle vaccines for universal influenza a protection

Authors: Louis Bourlon, Jessica Morlieras, Patrice N. Marche, Fabrice P. Navarro
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: Influenza A virus poses ongoing global health and economic challenges due to its antigenic variability and pandemic potential. Current seasonal vaccines targeting the variable hemagglutinin (HA) provide limited and strain-specific protection. The conserved Matrix 2 Ectodomain (M2e) offers a promising universal vaccine target, eliciting protection by targeting infected cells rather than neutralizing virions. However, the mechanism underlying protection mediated by this simple antigen is complex, and no M2e-based vaccine has been licensed yet. AREA COVERED: Precise M2e-vaccine optimization is needed. This review provides in-depth description of M2e established knowledge, as well as comparative studies using M2e-based vaccines. From this, the 'ideal archetype' of a universal vaccine including M2e will be defined. Finally, a review of the current preclinical nanoparticle based M2e vaccine will be provided, described, and analyzed. EXPERT OPINION: Optimizing antigen presentation, evaluating Fc-mediated effector functions with standardized assays, and integrating M2e into multivalent nanoparticle platforms targeting complementary conserved antigens are likely to be key steps toward achieving broad, durable, and clinically translatable protection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Influenza A virus poses ongoing global health and economic challenges due to its antigenic variability and pandemic potential. Current seasonal vaccines targeting the variable hemagglutinin (HA) provide limited and strain-specific protection. The conserved Matrix 2 Ectodomain (M2e) offers a promising universal vaccine target, eliciting protection by targeting infected cells rather than neutralizing virions. The review aims to define the 'ideal archetype' of a universal vaccine including M2e and provide a review of the current preclinical nanoparticle based M2e vaccine.
Evidence base and method
This review provides in-depth description of M2e established knowledge, as well as comparative studies using M2e-based vaccines. The review includes a discussion of the mechanism underlying protection mediated by this simple antigen and outlines the need for precise M2e-vaccine optimization.
Consensus and conclusions
However, the mechanism underlying protection mediated by this simple antigen is complex, and no M2e-based vaccine has been licensed yet. The review defines the 'ideal archetype' of a universal vaccine including M2e.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights the need for precise M2e-vaccine optimization and the importance of evaluating Fc-mediated effector functions with standardized assays.
Research and practice implications
Optimizing antigen presentation, evaluating Fc-mediated effector functions with standardized assays, and integrating M2e into multivalent nanoparticle platforms targeting complementary conserved antigens are likely to be key steps toward achieving broad, durable, and clinically translatable protection.

Emerging threat of avian influenza to the world's vultures

Emerging threat of avian influenza to the world's vultures

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

印度医学研究理事会:印度未发现新H1N1毒株;季节性流感病毒无需恐慌——《印度时报》

'Mostly self-limiting': No new H1N1 strain in India, ICMR says; seasonal flu virus poses no cause for pan - The Times of India
新闻简报印度医学研究理事会(ICMR)于2026年8月23日澄清,印度未发现新型甲型H1N1流感病毒毒株,目前流行的季节性流感病毒不会引发恐慌。报告指出,当前流行的H1N1病毒与甲型密苏里/11/2025(H1N1)pdm09样毒株相似,属于进化分支6B.1A.5a2a及其亚分支D.3.1.1,自2025年以来已在流行。这些病毒与北半球推荐的疫苗毒株高度匹配。季节性流感(包括H1N1)被描述为“多为自限性”,症状包括发热、咳嗽、头痛和全身疼痛,大多数患者在适当护理和休息后可康复。然而,幼儿、老年人及有基础疾病的脆弱人群可能面临并发症风险。ICMR强调,当前形势正通过常规监测密切跟踪,并已采取必要的公共卫生措施。德里卫生部长Pankaj Kumar Singh表示,德里市已全面准备应对此次疫情,床位、医生、药品及基本医疗设备均无短缺。报告强调,如症状持续或加重,应及时就医。
查看新闻五要素
时间
ICMR于2026年8月23日澄清情况,且该病毒自2025年以来已在流行。
地点与对象
该报告聚焦印度,特别是德里,涉及普通人群,并重点关注幼儿、老年人及有基础疾病等脆弱人群。
事件
印度医学研究理事会(ICMR)称,印度未发现新型甲型H1N1流感病毒毒株,目前流行的季节性流感病毒不会引发恐慌。报告还指出,当前流行的病毒与北半球推荐的疫苗毒株高度匹配。
规模、影响与风险
季节性流感(包括H1N1)被描述为“多为自限性”。常见症状包括发热、咳嗽、头痛和全身疼痛,大多数患者在适当护理和休息后可康复。然而,脆弱人群可能面临更高的并发症风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
ICMR正通过常规监测密切跟踪当前形势,并表示已采取必要的公共卫生措施。德里卫生部长Pankaj Kumar Singh向联邦卫生部长通报了持续监测及医院准备情况,表明德里市已全面准备应对此次疫情。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

'Mostly self-limiting': No new H1N1 strain in India, ICMR says; seasonal flu virus poses no cause for pan - The Times of India

News Brief
The Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) clarified on August 23, 2026, that no new strain of Influenza A (H1N1) has been identified in India, and the seasonal influenza virus currently in circulation poses no cause for panic. The report states that the circulating H1N1 viruses are similar to the A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1) pdm09-like strain and belong to clade 6B.1A.5a2a and subclade D.3.1.1, having been in circulation since 2025. These viruses are well matched with the vaccine strains recommended for the Northern Hemisphere. Seasonal influenza, including H1N1, is described as "mostly self-limiting" with symptoms like fever, cough, headache, and body aches, and most people recover with appropriate care and rest. However, vulnerable groups such as young children, elderly individuals, and those with underlying medical conditions may face higher risks of complications. The ICMR emphasized that the situation is being closely monitored through regular surveillance, and necessary public health measures are in place. Delhi Health Minister Pankaj Kumar Singh reported that the city is fully prepared to handle the current health situation, with no shortage of beds, doctors, medicines, or essential medical equipment. The report highlights the importance of consulting a doctor if symptoms persist or worsen.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

ICMR: No new H1N1 strain in India, surge is seasonal flu - The Times of India

News Brief
The Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) has reassured the public that no new H1N1 strain has been detected in India, attributing the recent surge in cases to seasonal influenza. The report highlights that the circulating H1N1 viruses are seasonal strains well-matched with vaccine strains recommended for the northern hemisphere. As of August 20, 2026, Delhi recorded 1,777 H1N1 cases out of 2,392 Influenza A cases, making H1N1 the predominant sub-type. The ICMR emphasized that the situation is being closely monitored through regular surveillance. Health officials have also advised that patients experiencing severe symptoms such as breathlessness, chest pain, or persistent fever should seek medical attention promptly. This information was reported by The Times of India on August 24, 2026, and is based on the latest findings from ICMR scientists.
Original source text (English)

未发现新H1N1毒株;当前印度流感病例上升系季节性增长——前WHO首席科学家如是说 - ANI新闻

No new H1N1 strain identified; what we're seeing is seasonal influenza uptick: Former WHO Chief Scientist - ANI News
新闻简报ANI新闻于2026年8月24日发布的新闻报道讨论了印度当前甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09病例的上升,该现象被归因于季节性增长而非新毒株。前WHO首席科学家Soumya Swaminathan博士表示,此次增长与往年情况相似,无需恐慌。印度医学研究理事会(ICMR)确认未发现新的甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09毒株,且流行株与北半球推荐的季节性流感疫苗株高度匹配。报道指出,病例上升主要源于检测能力和意识的提升,导致报告更加准确。报道同时提醒,大多数病例为自限性,但高风险人群(65岁以上、5岁以下、孕妇及免疫功能低下者)应采取预防措施。当前情况仍在调查中,未发现新变异株或传播力增强的证据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报道发布于2026年8月24日。事件日期未明确说明,但此次季节性增长被认为始于今年早些时候,并预计将持续至冬季。
地点与对象
报道提及印度多个城市,包括德里和班加罗尔,当地检测能力、意识和诊断水平的提升导致报告病例数增加。受影响人群包括高风险并发症人群,如65岁以上、5岁以下、孕妇及免疫功能低下者。
事件
前WHO首席科学家Soumya Swaminathan博士表示,当前甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09病例的增长为季节性增长,而非新毒株所致。印度医学研究理事会(ICMR)确认该国未发现新的甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09毒株。
规模、影响与风险
报道显示德里和班加罗尔等城市报告病例数增加,主要归因于检测能力、意识和诊断水平的提升。报道指出,一般人群风险较低,仅高风险人群需谨慎。流感病毒株与北半球推荐的季节性流感疫苗株高度匹配。
应对、状态与不确定性
当前情况仍在调查中,ICMR与Swaminathan博士强调,流感病例为季节性而非新毒株所致。应对措施包括向公众提供安抚信息,强调大多数病例为自限性,仅需休息和基本护理。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

No new H1N1 strain identified; what we're seeing is seasonal influenza uptick: Former WHO Chief Scientist - ANI News

News Brief
The news report from ANI News, published on August 24, 2026, discusses the current rise in H1N1 influenza cases in India, which is attributed to a seasonal uptick rather than a new strain. Dr. Soumya Swaminathan, former WHO Chief Scientist, stated that the increase is similar to previous years and is not cause for panic. The Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) confirmed that no new H1N1 strain has been identified, and the circulating viruses are well-matched with the vaccine strains recommended for the Northern Hemisphere. The report highlights that the rise in cases is due to improved surveillance and awareness, leading to more accurate reporting. It also notes that while most cases are self-limiting, high-risk groups such as those over 65, under 5, pregnant women, and individuals with weakened immune systems should take precautions. The situation remains under investigation, with no evidence of a new variant or increased transmissibility.
Original source text (English)
No new H1N1 strain identified; what we're seeing is seasonal influenza uptick: Former WHO Chief Scientist By Shalini Bhardwaj | Updated: Aug 24, 2026 21:19 IST ANI News Join our Whatsapp channel Chennai (Tamil Nadu) [India], August 24 (ANI): Amid concerns over the rising number of H1N1 influenza cases in several cities, former World Health Organisation (WHO) Chief Scientist Dr Soumya Swaminathan has informed that the current increase in H1N1 cases is a seasonal influenza uptick and there is no cause for panic, as no new strain has been detected. In an exclusive interview with ANI on Sunday on the ICMR report of no new virus circulating, Dr Swaminathan said that influenza cases usually increase during the monsoon season in India and continue into winter, adding that improved surveillance systems have helped in identifying circulating viruses more effectively. "I think what we're seeing is a seasonal influenza uptick. We see this every year. The doctors feel that this year it has started a little earlier, but normally in India, we do have a monsoon seasonal increase in influenza cases, and then it continues into winter," she said. "Thanks to better surveillance through the government ICMR viral laboratories throughout the year, we actually now know which viruses are circulating, which are predominant, and we can also correlate with clinical presentation, with severity, and with hospital admissions. This is a very good resource," she added. Dr Swaminathan said the present rise in cases is linked to the H1N1 Michigan strain, which has been circulating since the 2009 pandemic, and not due to the emergence of any new variant. "The current increase in cases is because of H1N1, a Michigan strain. It's an influenza A virus, and it's the same strain that has been circulating since the pandemic in 2009, very similar to what was last year. I think we need to reassure people that this is not a new strain. It is not more transmissible or more virulent," she added. She further said that increased awareness, testing and diagnostic facilities have resulted in more reported cases from cities including Delhi and Bengaluru. "I think there's a lot more awareness, more testing, more diagnostics being performed, and therefore more cases are being reported from many hospitals in Delhi, Bengaluru, and other cities. People might tend to panic, thinking that, you know, there are suddenly thousands of cases. It's just better testing, but also an increase in the number of people who are down with H1N1 at this particular time," Dr Swaminathan said. "Really nothing to worry about, except for those who are at very high risk of complications," she added. Talking about symptoms of H1N1 and influenza, Dr Swaminathan said patients usually experience fever, body ache, sore throat, headache and fatigue, with most recovering within a week. "The symptoms of H1N1 or any other influenza or flu as we call it are fever, body ache, sore throat, headache, fatigue and the symptoms usually last for 4-5 days, maybe up to a week at the most. Most people tend to recover with just rest, paracetamol, and plenty of hot fluids," she added. Dr Swaminathan also highlighted that certain groups remain at higher risk of developing severe complications, particularly pneumonia. "The people who are at risk of going in for more severe complications, particularly pneumonia, are those who are above the age of 65, children under the age of 5, pregnant women, and also those whose immune system is not working that well," she said. She explained that people with weakened immune systems, including those undergoing cancer chemotherapy, suffering from autoimmune diseases or taking corticosteroids for a long period, need to be more cautious. "These are also people who've had cancer chemotherapy, for example, or have autoimmune disease, or have been on corticosteroids for a long time, and because the immune system is a little bit weak in these people, it could flare up and become pneumonia," Dr Swaminathan said. Meanwhile, Scientists at the Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) have clarified that no new strain of Influenza A (H1N1) has been identified in the country and said there is no cause for panic over the seasonal influenza virus currently in circulation. A senior ICMR scientist said on Sunday, "The influenza A (H1N1) pdm09 viruses currently circulating in India are similar to the A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1) pdm09-like virus strain. These viruses belong to clade 6B.1A.5a2a and subclade D.3.1.1 and have been in circulation since 2025." As per the ICMR, the scientists also clarified that the influenza strains currently circulating are well matched with the vaccine strains recommended for the Northern Hemisphere. Seasonal influenza , including H1N1, is "mostly self-limiting". Common symptoms include fever, cough, headache and body aches, and most people recover with appropriate care and adequate rest, the ICMR added in their statement. (ANI)
View five news elements
Time
The report was published on August 24, 2026. The event date is not explicitly stated, but the seasonal influenza uptick is noted to have started earlier this year and is expected to continue into winter.
Location and population
The report mentions cities in India, including Delhi and Bengaluru, where increased awareness, testing, and diagnostics have led to more reported cases. The affected population includes individuals at high risk of complications, such as those over 65, under 5, pregnant women, and people with weakened immune systems.
Event
Former WHO Chief Scientist Dr. Soumya Swaminathan stated that the current increase in H1N1 cases is a seasonal influenza uptick, not due to a new strain. The Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) confirmed that no new strain of Influenza A (H1N1) has been identified in the country.
Scale, impact and risk
The report indicates an increase in reported cases in cities like Delhi and Bengaluru, attributed to better awareness, testing, and diagnostics. The risk is described as low for the general population, with only high-risk groups needing caution. The influenza strains are well-matched with the vaccine strains recommended for the Northern Hemisphere.
Response, status and uncertainty
The situation is under investigation, with the ICMR and Dr. Swaminathan emphasizing that the current influenza cases are seasonal and not caused by a new strain. The response includes reassurance to the public, highlighting that most cases are self-limiting and require rest and basic care.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

季节性流感,更为凶猛的暴发——《印度时报》

A seasonal flu, a sharper surge - The Times of India
新闻简报《印度时报》于2026年8月23日发布的文章报道,印度德里地区甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09感染病例显著上升。医生注意到就诊患者中出现发热、咳嗽及类感冒症状的病例增多,2026年累计报告1,777例,而去年同期仅为229例。此次暴发被归因于季风相关湿度、室内拥挤及学校和工作场所的密切接触。老年人、幼儿及慢性病患者等脆弱人群出现并发症的风险更高。医务人员建议避免自行使用诊断及自行服药,如症状持续应就医,并建议采取佩戴口罩、勤洗手及生病时居家等预防措施。可通过咽拭子或鼻拭子进行甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09检测,高风险人群建议使用抗病毒药物药物。文章强调及时就医的重要性,并指出印度流感的季节性特征,其高峰受天气模式驱动。
查看新闻五要素
时间
医生报告门诊接诊大量出现发热、咳嗽及类感冒症状的患者。
地点与对象
印度德里;出现发热、咳嗽及类感冒症状的患者。
事件
医生报告印度德里地区甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09感染病例及相关症状增加,重点关注发热、咳嗽及类感冒症状。该事件已通过医学观察及检测确认。
规模、影响与风险
德里2026年累计报告甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09感染病例1,777例,而去年同期为229例。感染传播与季风相关湿度、室内拥挤及学校和工作场所的密切接触有关。老年人、幼儿及慢性病患者等脆弱人群出现并发症的风险更高。
应对、状态与不确定性
医生建议患者避免自行使用诊断及自行服药,如症状持续应就医,并采取佩戴口罩、勤洗手及生病时居家等预防措施。可通过咽拭子或鼻拭子进行检测,高风险人群建议使用抗病毒药物药物。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

A seasonal flu, a sharper surge - The Times of India

News Brief
The article from The Times of India, published on August 23, 2026, reports a significant rise in H1N1 influenza cases in Delhi, India. Doctors note an increase in patients presenting with fever, cough, and cold-like symptoms, with 1,777 cases recorded in 2026 compared to 229 in the same period last year. The surge is attributed to monsoon-related humidity, indoor overcrowding, and close contact in schools and workplaces. Vulnerable groups, including older adults, young children, and individuals with chronic illnesses, are at higher risk of complications. Medical professionals advise against self-diagnosis and self-medication, recommend consulting a doctor for persistent symptoms, and suggest preventive measures such as wearing masks, washing hands, and staying home when unwell. Testing for H1N1 is available through throat or nasal swabs, and antiviral medication is advised for high-risk individuals. The article emphasizes the importance of timely medical attention and highlights the seasonal nature of influenza in India, with peaks driven by weather patterns.

If you develop fever, cough or cold symptoms, avoid self-diagnosis and self-medication, say doctors 1 2 Amid the monsoon and changing weather conditions, NCR is witnessing an increase in H1N1, commonly known as swine flu, along with H3N2 and other viruses. Delhi has recorded 1,777 H1N1 cases so far in 2026, compared with 229 during the corresponding period last year. Doctors report seeing a large number of patients in OPDs with fever, cough and cold-like symptoms. They share medical advice on what to do if you feel sick and why getting a flu shot after discussing it with your doctor might be worth considering.“We are currently witnessing a significant rise in H1N1 influenza, commonly known as swine flu. In our OPD, a large proportion of patients with fever, cough and cold-like symptoms are testing positive for H1N1. The infection can be confirmed through a throat or nasal swab. Patients with severe symptoms or underlying health conditions may require closer medical attention,” comments Dr Rakesh Gupta, Senior Consultant, Internal Medicine, Indraprastha Apollo Hospital, Delhi.Doctors warn against self-diagnosisIf you develop fever, cough or cold symptoms, avoid self-diagnosis and self-medication.“Consult a doctor, especially if symptoms persist or worsen. by Taboolaby Taboola Sponsored LinksSponsored Links Promoted LinksPromoted Links You May Like My Knife Feels Like a Scalpel After Using This Electric SharpenerBesyner Learn More Undo It is also important to stay away from others and limit close contact to prevent spreading the infection. High-risk individuals should seek medical advice without delay,” advises Dr Rakesh Gupta.Dr Manav Manchanda, Director and Head of Respiratory, Critical Care & Sleep Medicine, Asian Hospital, adds, "In my experience, the increase is more pronounced in OPDs where most patients are discharged home without hospital admission. But vulnerable patients can have complications and may need to be admitted. In India, influenza can circulate year-round, with seasonal peaks driven by weather patterns. Increased transmission can be attributed to monsoon-related humidity, indoor overcrowding, exposure at school and the workplace, and close family contact."'Most healthy people recover in about a week with rest'My biggest piece of advice is not to panic but also not to ignore symptoms, shares Dr Manav Manchanda. "Most healthy people recover in about a week with rest, fluids and symptomatic treatment. But do not ignore symptoms that get worse. Seek medical attention if you have persistent or recurrent fever, difficulty breathing, chest pain, confusion, dehydration or dropping oxygen levels. Older adults, young children, pregnant women and people with chronic illnesses should seek medical advice sooner," says the doctor.'Do not try to push through the illness'Advising people not to push through the illness, Dr Neetu Jain, Senior Consultant Pulmonology, Critical Care and Sleep Medicine, PSRI Hospital, shares that one should give their body adequate rest and fluids. "Give your body adequate rest and fluids and monitor how your symptoms are progressing. Fever, body ache and other symptoms can usually be managed with appropriate medication after consulting a doctor. One common mistake is taking antibiotics without medical advice. Influenza is caused by a virus, so antibiotics do not treat it. People at higher risk of complications may require antiviral medication, and these medicines are generally more effective when started early."Flu vaccine is particularly beneficial for vulnerable groups, including the elderly, young children and people with underlying health conditions. Even those who have previously had the flu should discuss vaccination with their doctor Dr Rakesh GuptaPrecautions to takeIf you are unwell, stay home and avoid close contact with others, especially elderly people, young children, pregnant women and those with conditions such as diabetes, asthma or heart diseaseWearing a well-fitting mask in crowded or poorly ventilated places can provide additional protectionRegular handwashing, covering your mouth and nose while coughing or sneezing, avoiding touching your face and keeping indoor spaces well ventilated can also reduce transmissionAdequate sleep, hydration and a balanced diet are important for maintaining overall health- Dr Neetu Jain Looking for trusted parenting advice? B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

印度博帕尔密切关注季节性流感病例——《印度时报》

Seasonal influenza cases under close watch in Bhopal - The Times of India
新闻简报印度博帕尔卫生当局正密切监测季节性流感病例,特别是甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09,在过去一周内报告了3例病例,使8月份总病例数增至6例。官方重点关注出现持续咳嗽、感冒和呼吸困难等症状的患者。尽管当前形势未被视为严峻,但已敦促高风险人群(包括老年人、孕妇及持续季节性疾病患者)就医咨询。强调对疑似病例进行检测以实现及时治疗并评估社区传播。医疗机构已加强筛查流程,并配备隔离设施。联邦政府已指示各州与医院和实验室协调,以实现有效的监测和应对准备。该报告未提供有关传播或风险水平的进一步详情。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年8月24日 01:18 IST
地点与对象
印度中央邦博帕尔;高风险人群包括老年人、孕妇及持续季节性疾病患者
事件
博帕尔卫生当局在过去一周内报告3例甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09病例,使8月份总病例数增至6例,并已加强对季节性流感(包括甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09)的监测。官方正监测出现持续咳嗽、感冒和呼吸困难等症状的患者。
规模、影响与风险
过去一周报告3例甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09病例,8月份总病例数为6例。官方称当前形势未构成严峻威胁,但需采取预防措施。检测疑似病例对评估社区传播至关重要。医疗机构已强化筛查流程并做好隔离设施准备。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生当局正监测出现持续症状的患者、检测疑似病例并强化筛查流程。隔离设施已准备就绪。官方强调需采取预防措施,但未显示出现警示信号。联邦政府已要求各州与医院和实验室协调,以实现有效的监测和应对准备。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seasonal influenza cases under close watch in Bhopal - The Times of India

News Brief
Health authorities in Bhopal, India, are closely monitoring seasonal influenza cases, particularly H1N1, following the reporting of three cases in the past week, which brings the total number of cases in August to six. Officials are focusing on patients exhibiting prolonged symptoms such as cough, cold, and breathing difficulties. While the situation is not considered alarming, they have urged high-risk groups, including the elderly, pregnant women, and those with persistent seasonal illnesses, to consult healthcare professionals. Testing suspected cases is emphasized for timely treatment and assessing community transmission. Medical facilities have enhanced screening protocols and are prepared with isolation facilities. The Union government has directed states to coordinate with hospitals and laboratories for effective surveillance and preparedness. No further details on the spread or risk level were provided in the report.

Medium Large probe Bhopal: Health authorities in the state capital have stepped up surveillance for seasonal influenza, including H1N1, after three H1N1 cases were reported in the city over the past week, taking the total number of cases reported in August to six, according to sources.Officials and hospitals are monitoring patients with prolonged symptoms such as cough, cold and breathing difficulties, which can persist during seasonal influenza infections. H1N1 cases generally increase during the monsoon and winter months.“There is no cause for alarm. However, people in high-risk categories, including the elderly, pregnant women and those with persistent seasonal illnesses, should consult healthcare professionals,” said Dr Lokendra Dave, professor of respiratory medicine at Gandhi Medical College (GMC), Bhopal.Dave stressed the importance of timely treatment and cautioned people against self-medication. “Precaution is needed, but the situation is not alarming,” he said.Testing suspected cases is important not only for timely treatment but also for assessing the extent of community-level transmission, officials said.Medical facilities have strengthened screening protocols and kept isolation facilities ready for suspected cases. by Taboolaby Taboola Sponsored LinksSponsored Links Promoted LinksPromoted Links You May Like Brain Fog? CMHO Bhopal when contacted was not available to provide latest updates.The Union government has also asked states and Union Territories to coordinate with hospitals, laboratories and other stakeholders to ensure effective surveillance, early detection, timely clinical management and preparedness for seasonal influenza, including H1N1.The Centre has called for a proactive approach by state governments, with local bodies playing a role in spreading awareness about prevention and control of seasonal diseases. Time
Aug 24, 2026, 01:18 IST.
Location and population
Bhopal, Madhya Pradesh, India; high-risk categories including the elderly, pregnant women, and those with persistent seasonal illnesses.
Event
Health authorities in Bhopal have increased surveillance for seasonal influenza, including H1N1, after three H1N1 cases were reported in the city over the past week, bringing the total number of cases in August to six. Officials are monitoring patients with prolonged symptoms such as cough, cold, and breathing difficulties.
Scale, impact and risk
Three H1N1 cases reported in the past week, total of six cases in August. Officials stated that the situation is not alarming, but precaution is needed. Testing suspected cases is important for assessing community-level transmission. Medical facilities have strengthened screening protocols and kept isolation facilities ready.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health authorities are monitoring patients with prolonged symptoms, testing suspected cases, and strengthening screening protocols. Isolation facilities are prepared. Officials emphasized the need for precaution but noted no cause for alarm. The Union government has asked states to coordinate with hospitals and laboratories for effective surveillance and preparedness.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Nadda 评估猪流感与 季节性流感 防控工作,强调警惕与准备 - ETV Bharat

Nadda Reviews Swine Flu, Seasonal Influenza Vigilance And Preparedness - ETV Bharat
新闻简报2026年8月21日,印度联邦卫生部长J P Nadda 评估了针对甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09及季节性流感的防控准备工作,重点关注德里及其他邦。会议由德里邦卫生部长Pankaj Kumar Singh出席,评估了全国监测与流行病学形势,包括类流感(ILI)与严重急性呼吸道感染(SARI)的趋势。官员们强调需持续警惕、加强监测能力并做好应对病例激增的准备。卫生部强调将类流感与SARI趋势作为呼吸道感染的关键指标进行监测,并重视公众意识与预防健康措施以控制传播。当前形势仍在密切监控中,监测与准备工作丝毫未放松。此次评估凸显中央政府对季节性流感的主动预防措施而非被动响应的重视。
查看新闻五要素
时间
评估会议于2026年8月21日举行,发布日期与会议同步。
地点与对象
会议重点关注德里及全国多个邦,德里的医疗设施是审查的关键领域。
事件
印度联邦卫生部长J P Nadda 在德里卫生部长Pankaj Kumar Singh陪同下,评估了全国针对甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09及季节性流感的防控准备工作。
规模、影响与风险
会议审查了检测与实验室监测机制,以及医疗设施应对季节性流感病例(包括甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09感染)的准备情况。官员们强调将类流感与SARI趋势作为呼吸道感染的指标进行监测的重要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
当前形势正在持续密切监控中,监测与准备工作丝毫未放松。卫生部正与各邦、中央直辖区、医院及实验室协调,以强化监测能力并确保及时临床管理。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nadda Reviews Swine Flu, Seasonal Influenza Vigilance And Preparedness - ETV Bharat

News Brief
On August 21, 2026, Union Health Minister J P Nadda reviewed the preparedness for H1N1 influenza and seasonal influenza in India, with a focus on Delhi and other states. The meeting, attended by Delhi Health Minister Pankaj Kumar Singh, assessed the national surveillance and epidemiological situation, including trends in influenza-like illness (ILI) and severe acute respiratory infection (SARI). Officials highlighted the need for continued vigilance, robust surveillance, and readiness to handle any increase in cases. The Health Ministry emphasized monitoring ILI and SARI trends as key indicators for respiratory infections and stressed the importance of public awareness and preventive health practices to limit transmission. The situation remains under close monitoring, with no let-up in surveillance and preparedness efforts. The review underscores the Centre's focus on proactive measures rather than reactive responses to seasonal influenza outbreaks.
Original source text (English)
supreme courtrahul gandhiamit shahbjp E हिं অ বাং ગુ ಕ മ म ଓ ਪੰ த తె ار National English हिंदी उत्तर प्रदेशउत्तराखंडछत्तीसगढ़झारखंडदिल्लीबिहारमध्य प्रदेशराजस्थानहरियाणाहिमाचल प्रदेश অসমীয়া বাংলা ગુજરાતી ಕನ್ನಡ മലയാളം मराठी ଓଡିଆ ਪੰਜਾਬੀ தமிழ் తెలుగు ఆంధ్రప్రదేశ్తెలంగాణ اردو Live NATIONAL WORLD SPORTS ENTERTAINMENT OPINION PHOTOS HEALTH STATE BUSINESS More Live ETV Bharat / bharat Nadda Reviews Swine Flu, Seasonal Influenza Vigilance And Preparedness Delhi health facilities reviewed as Centre stresses surveillance, testing and readiness to handle seasonal influenza cases, reports Gautam Debroy. Union Health Minister J P Nadda at H1N1 preparedness review meeting (Courtesy: Special Arrangement (Health Ministry)) By ETV Bharat English Team Published : August 21, 2026 at 5:08 PM IST 2 Min Read New Delhi: Union Health Minister J P Nadda on Friday reviewed the prevailing situation and preparedness for H1N1 influenza (commonly known as swine flu) in the country, with a particular focus on the National Capital, amid the seasonal circulation of influenza viruses. Delhi Health Minister Pankaj Kumar Singh also attended the meeting, during which the Union Health Ministry assessed the national surveillance and epidemiological situation and reviewed trends in influenza-like illness (ILI) and severe acute respiratory infection (SARI). The meeting also examined testing and laboratory surveillance mechanisms, besides the preparedness of healthcare facilities to detect and manage seasonal influenza cases, including H1N1 infections. Nadda emphasised the need for continued vigilance and robust surveillance, directing that influenza trends be monitored closely so that any emerging pattern can be detected early and appropriate measures taken. The preparedness of healthcare facilities in Delhi was a key focus of the review. The availability of hospital beds, critical care facilities and essential medical supplies was assessed to ensure that hospitals remain equipped to deal with any increase in cases. The meeting assumes significance as Delhi and several states across the country have been reporting increasing number of seasonal influenza. Seasonal influenza cause mild respiratory symptoms to severe disease requiring hospitalisation and critical care, particularly among vulnerable patients. The Health Minister also stressed the importance of public awareness and preventive health practices to limit transmission. People were advised to follow respiratory hygiene and hand hygiene and take appropriate precautions when experiencing respiratory symptoms. Nadda specifically advised individuals with persistent or severe respiratory symptoms, breathing difficulty or other warning signs to seek medical advice promptly rather than delaying treatment. Officials present in the meeting said that the situation is being closely and continuously monitored and that there is no let-up in surveillance and preparedness efforts. "The Centre is coordinating with states and Union Territories, hospitals, laboratories and other stakeholders to strengthen surveillance, facilitate early detection and ensure timely clinical management of influenza cases," an official said. It was informed in the meeting that health ministry is also keeping a close watch on ILI and SARI trends, which serve as important indicators for monitoring respiratory infections and identifying any unusual increase that may require enhanced public-health intervention. 'Today's review highlighted the Centre's emphasis on preparedness rather than a reactive approach, with health authorities being asked to maintain adequate hospitality capacity, testing facilities and essential supplies," the official said. The Ministry reiterated that timely detection, appropriate clinical management and public adherence to basic preventive measures remain crucial to limiting the impact of seasonal influenza, including H1N1.
